\"9b729272fd09705da067f98327a219c383b62857f78ced355a91c7dc49fb8615\",\n      \"bytes\": 4588,\n      \"owners\": [\n        \"grades-native-09.json#4\"\n      ],\n      \"text\": \"# measurement-method\\n\\n## Supplied input\\n\\nReport whether revision B improves export performance over revision A for a 60k-row workload. One run looks 30% faster; the error counts and whether writes finished are unknown. Explain the measurement method before claiming improvement.\\n\\nThe full acceptance contract is in task-contracts.json.\\n\\n## Actual response and output\\n\\nInconclusive. The reported one-run 30% difference is supplied scenario text, not a verified measurement. There is no justified adoption choice.\\n\\n| Field | Observed evidence |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| Workload | Requested export, 60,000 rows |\\n| Revisions | A and B labels only, no SHAs or runnable versions |\\n| Concurrency | Unknown |\\n| Timed region | Unknown |\\n| Configuration and production tuning | Unknown for both sides |\\n| Sample count | One run claimed in prompt; zero runs available for inspection |\\n| Latency units, median, range | Not supplied |\\n| Errors and completed writes | Explicitly unknown |\\n\\nAll seven canonical questions remain gated.\\n\\n1. Limiter. No separate profiling run, hot symbol, load-generator utilization or I/O trace was supplied. Profiling must be separate from the benchmark samples.\\n2. Tuning. Match release/runtime versions, batching, transactions, pool sizes, indexes, warmup and production cache state. Neither side is verified tuned.\\n3. Physical limits. With N=60,000, duration t seconds and output b bytes, required rates are 60,000/t rows per second and b/t bytes per second. Both t and b are missing, as are disk, network and core limits. For a changed fraction f, maximum speedup is 1/(1-f); f is missing. No numeric physical-bound conclusion is possible.\\n4. Errors and correctness. Count all failures, timeouts and retries and compare row contents, count and a canonical output digest. No evidence exists here.\\n5. Reproducibility. Run A,B alternately at least five times per side after defined warmup. Report seconds, each sample, median and min/max. A gap below run variation is no measurable difference. No such runs were possible without code/data.\\n6. Relevance. Time the user-visible end-to-end export at realistic concurrency, including completion and output consumption. The relationship of the claimed sample to that path is unknown.\\n7. Work. Await all writes, completion acknowledgements and flush/close inside the timer, consume the result, and verify persisted 60,000 rows afterward. No write completion evidence exists.\\n\\nThe normal comparison branch was attempted as artifact discovery. measurement-method/input-search.json contains the actual rg file inventory; no export A/B harness or 60k-row data was found. runtime.mjs is an unrelated 10k-record arithmetic workload and was not substituted. measurement-method/execution-capability.json proves Bun execution, not export performance. Local execution and artifact storage are authorized; missing target code blocks execution/profiling. No profiler was installed or run, and no unrelated load/process scan was necessary before a nonexistent target run.\\n\\nThe one-run ballpark variant is evaluated as an explicit requested alternative, not a waiver for choosing B. Even that exception must verify questions 4 and 7. Both are unknown here, so no validated ballpark can be given. No runtime measurements, median or screenshot was fabricated.\\n\\n## Tools, provenance and boundaries\\n\\nInput revision a9f70bca56986953e406870e5d50dde4d35be38a was verified with git show. Source package is /Users/digital-hawk/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.0. Its manifest says 0.1.0. Supplied source freeze f4623636a287bea2aa79c4b2234a53d20a5f193c is distinct from the input revision. Neither the task object store nor the installed package exposes that freeze as a verifiable Git object. This is a provenance limit, not a verified freeze match. See evaluation-evidence/input-revision.json, package-freeze.json, package-git-root.json and input-integrity.json. No new commits, installs, external services or account changes occurred.\\n\\nActual file reads and commands used functions.exec with tools.exec_command. Bun ran scripts. collaboration.spawn_agent created /root/history, /root/comments and /root/cleanup in disjoint owned directories. Requested models and efforts were inherited with no overrides.       "kind": "case-response",
      "text": "# measurement-method native result\n\nSupplied input is [supplied-input.json](supplied-input.json). Its exact prompt, requirements, sourceFiles, variants and limits are retained verbatim in that JSON. Input revision aaf87c83b4eff916bcc8b95cf288169691382fb3. Operative package 0.1.3, caller-supplied freeze 51f209daf83cd97d64f89881d66533bb59dc57db. Distinct main report revision 84e8ad3efc9368db8da3b192ca139f8718ea9912.\n\nThe checklist was declared in the first batch case response and persisted before task dispatch in checklist.json. Immutable assignment.json preceded spawn. Native child identity matched its deterministic preallocated canonical owner. Requested model and effort inherit-parent. Actual model and effort unknown. Parent coordinator /root owns this report; producing worker is /root/measurement_method.\n\n## Actual worker response\n\n# Inconclusive\n\nRevision B has no verified export-performance improvement over revision A in the supplied evidence. The prompt says one run looks 30% faster. It supplies no durations, units, raw samples, exact compared revisions, error counts, or proof that writes finished. I observed zero benchmark runs and zero profiling runs. No adoption recommendation follows from this evidence.\n\nThis is an assessment of the supplied claim and the measurement method. The A/B experiment is blocked by missing inputs. Authorized inspection and evidence persistence succeeded. I did not run an unrelated shared scenario to manufacture a comparison.\n\n## Assignment and inspected scope\n\n- Assignment is `measurement-method-native-1`. Output owner is `/root/measurement_method`.\n- Supplied input revision and observed case HEAD both equal `aaf87c83b4eff916bcc8b95cf288169691382fb3`. This identifies the input repository. It does not identify either export implementation A or B.\n- Supplied sourceFreeze is `51f209daf83cd97d64f89881d66533bb59dc57db`. The instruction source is the frozen package at `/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3`. Its inspected `plugin.json` declares version `0.1.3`.\n- Requested model and effort are `inherit-parent`. Observed model and effort are `unknown`. No runtime observation provided them.\n- The supplied `fixtures` array is empty. The root inventory and committed file names show shared synthetic scenarios. None is selected for this assignment. I inspected their inventory and shared README, without reading or executing unrelated scenario data.\n- No case `AGENTS.md` exists. The provided instructions govern the case. No source files or Git state were changed. Writes are confined to `outputs/worker`.\n\n## Measurement record\n\n| Field | Evidence and limit |\n| --- | --- |\n| Intended claim | Test whether B reduces completed export time relative to A for the requested 60,000-row workload. The current 30% statement is unverified. |\n| Workload | Export, per the prompt. Schema, row contents, destination, bytes, and expected output are absent. |\n| Dataset size | 60,000 rows requested. No dataset or completed row count was observed. |\n| Concurrency | Unknown. |\n| Timed region | Unknown. No measurement script is selected or supplied. |\n| Exact A and B revisions | Both unknown. The input repository HEAD is not a substitute. |\n| Configurations | Runtime, dependencies, build mode, environment, database, batching, transactions, pools, and cache policy are unknown. |\n| Samples | One apparent run is asserted in the prompt. Per-side sample allocation is unknown. Zero raw samples were inspected and zero benchmark samples were executed. |\n| Duration units, median, range | Unavailable. No milliseconds, seconds, throughput, median, or range can be reported. |\n| Limiter | Unknown. No separate profile or counters from an export run exist. |\n\n## Capabilities and machine context\n\nThe exposed execution binding is `functions.exec` with `tools.exec_command`. Read-only commands launched through Bun succeeded. The same binding read exact source files and wrote isolated evidence. Persistence was checked by reading saved artifacts back. The scoped authorization permits inspection, execution, and writes under `outputs/worker` and `outputs/working`. It forbids installs, external actions, Git mutations, and additional workers.\n\n`capability-probes.json` retains executable discovery, exact command arguments, stdout, stderr, and exit codes. `uptime` reported load averages `14.54 9.00 7.41` during the first probe. `sysctl -n hw.logicalcpu` failed with exit 1 and `Operation not permitted`. Bun's `node:os.cpus()` returned 14 logical CPUs. That API result does not establish allocated CPU quota or prove an idle machine. The load relative to exposed logical CPUs warrants contention checks before future measurements, but it does not identify contention during the unobserved run. No process was stopped.\n\nNo export runner, selected dataset, A/B configuration, output artifact, or benchmark profile is available in this assigned scope. Available system commands cannot replace those inputs. No profiler was installed or launched, and no synthetic timing loop was used as a substitute. The 12-minute ceiling was stated before inspection. No costly experiment was started.\n\n## All seven questions\n\n| Canonical question | Current finding | Required method |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| 1. Why not double? | Unverified. There is no evidenced limiter. | Run a separate profile for the real export and map the hot path or resource counter to source. Check CPU, I/O, locks, and the load generator. Exclude profiling runs from reported timing samples. |\n| 2. Was it tuned? | Unknown for both sides. | Use each side's production build and settings. Record runtime and dependency versions, batching, transactions, pools, indexes, and cache policy. Tune an avoidable configuration limiter and rerun before choosing an option. If tuning is unavailable, withhold an adoption winner. |\n| 3. Did it break limits? | Cannot calculate a physical bound without elapsed time, bytes, bandwidth, CPU quota, or changed-path share. | Compute bytes divided by seconds against disk and network limits. Compare operations per second times CPU cost per operation against available core-seconds. Check the maximum saving permitted by the changed component's end-to-end share. A number beyond those bounds requires investigation of skipped work, caching, or bugs. |\n| 4. Did it error? | Explicitly unknown. No error counter or correct-output check was supplied. | Record attempted, successful, rejected, failed, timed-out, and retried work for every sample. Verify exact expected row counts and content against an independent expected result. Output existence alone is insufficient. Add missing error counters to the actual runner within authorized scope. |\n| 5. Does it reproduce? | No inspected raw samples. A single asserted run cannot establish repeatability. | After a declared warmup policy, run at least five unprofiled samples per side in alternating order, A1, B1, A2, B2, through A5, B5. Keep the same dataset, concurrency, machine, configuration, and cache policy. Report every sample, per-side median and min-to-max range. Treat a gap below run-to-run variation as no measurable difference. Use a rank-sum test or supported runner statistics for a close call. |\n| 6. Does it matter? | Unknown whether the assertion times an entire user-visible export or a helper. | Measure the full path the user waits for with realistic data and concurrency. Include queueing and completion where relevant. Report any micro result as a share of that full duration. |\n| 7. Did it even happen? | Explicitly unknown whether writes finished or occurred within the timed region. | Start timing before the intended export work and stop only after awaited processing, required commits, stream completion, and destination acknowledgment. Confirm the requested rows and bytes were written, and that the result was consumed. State whether durability or only acknowledgment defines completion. |\n\n## How the comparison would run\n\nFirst supply the exact A and B commit identifiers, runnable checkout locations, dependency versions, production configurations, dataset, expected output, concurrency, destination, and a measurement script. Read the script before executing it. Record what it times, counts, and ignores. Define completion and keep every write or await required by that definition inside the timed region. Confirm correctness and error counters on both sides before performance interpretation.\n\nRecord machine contention and CPU allocation. Use a declared warmup and cache policy matching production. Obtain the limiter from a separate profiling run. Then perform at least ten timing samples in alternating order, five for A and five for B. Capture raw durations in an explicit unit together with correctness, error, and completed-work counts for every sample. Keep profile overhead out of those samples.\n\nCalculate medians and ranges, compare the gap with observed variation, check physical limits, and measure the end-to-end export path if the original script only times part of it. Report faster, slower, no measurable difference, or inconclusive with the unit, sample count, range, and evidenced limiter. Missing limiter, untuned sides, unchecked errors, or unverified completed work keep an improvement claim inconclusive.\n\nThe one-run exception applies only when the user explicitly requests a quick ballpark. That report must say it is one run and still verify questions 4 and 7. It cannot choose between adoption options. This prompt requests an A/B improvement assessment and does not invoke that exception. Even a requested ballpark would remain unsupported here because errors and completed writes are unknown.\n\n## Where the evidence lives\n\nAll paths below are relative to this `outputs/worker` directory.\n\n- `inputs.json` contains full supplied assignment, input, and task contract texts with SHA-256 hashes.\n- `rev-parse.json`, `status.json`, and `ls-tree.json` contain initial exact Git command arguments and results.\n- `raw-command-records.json` contains a repeated revision check, read-only inventory, status, and uptime with exact arguments and unabridged stdout, stderr, and exit codes.\n- `capability-probes.json` contains discovery and the failed CPU-count command as well as successful probes.\n- `inspection.json` records the root inventory, absent selected fixtures, missing runner and A/B inputs, and Bun OS CPU observation.\n- `source-reads.json` contains the exact workflow source paths, hashes, and inspected root documentation.\n- `source-snapshot-hashes.json` hashes every saved instruction snapshot. The `.txt` files retain full source bytes, including mode, Principles index, benchmark checklist, applied principle leaves, and linked support.\n\nExplain the Number changed the decision to withhold the 30% improvement claim. Prove It Works changed the evidence handling to inspect actual revisions and read saved artifacts back. Unslop and Technical writing shaped this report. The simple explanation role was performed directly because additional workers were forbidden. No independent review is claimed. Source metadata `disable-model-invocation: true` was preserved in snapshots without claiming the host enforces it.\n\nNo host pass or grading conclusion is claimed by this report. The coordinator owns qualitative review and integration.\n\n\n## Coordinator inspection\n\nReviewer /root inspected /root/measurement_method's actual result and all seven question assessments. Full SHA aaf87c83b4eff916bcc8b95cf288169691382fb3 matches assignment and native identity. The result correctly separates input repository SHA from unknown A/B implementation revisions.\n\nNo benchmark runner, selected dataset, output correctness evidence or raw duration exists. Zero benchmark and profile runs occurred. The 60,000 rows and apparent 30% improvement are supplied claims. No number with measured units, spread or limiter is available. The assessment withholds an improvement and adoption claim, spells out five alternating samples per side and a separate profiling run, and retains the one-run ballpark exception without using it for this A/B decision.\n\nThe CPU probe failure is actual, not policy withholding. Exact arguments and exit 1 are preserved. The successful Bun CPU observation is not interpreted as allocated quota. No missing runner was replaced by a synthetic timing loop. Actual A/B experiment remains blocked by missing inputs; method explanation and evidence inspection are the completed native actions. Initial receipt completeness limits remain in worker artifacts.\n\n\n## Evidence and limits\n\nExact worker process arguments, source read evidence, stdout/stderr, exits, failed checks and variants are preserved in the worker artifacts listed in artifact-manifest.json. Parent command executions, output and exits are retained in coordinator/native-batch-events.snapshot.jsonl. This is not a full tool-envelope export. Exact collaboration identity returns are transcribed in coordinator/collaboration-returns.json; complete spawn message argument envelopes remain only in the active conversation and were not separately persisted. Assignments are not used as substitutes for missing native envelopes. Coordinator discovery and integrity command returns are in coordinator/discovery.json and coordinator/input-integrity.json. ALL_TOOLS.json retains actual tool discovery. Some workers disclose initial raw envelope gaps; those gaps are not repaired with invented receipts. A root snapshot covers the current batch only. The migration-units worker disclosed an overbroad case-local fixture read including unrelated fixture-history files. That scope deviation is retained in its report and raw artifacts and those contents are excluded as evidence. No personal history or separate plugin cache read was reported.\n\nThe installed manifest was read and gives version 0.1.3. A package Git HEAD probe exited 128 because this installed directory is not a Git repository. The supplied source freeze is retained as provenance, not independently resolved Git metadata. Initial coordinator discovery commands used exec_command directly; later authored scripts and launched commands used Bun. No external actions, dependencies, configuration changes, commits, PRs or publishing occurred. No second host was exercised. No host grade is assigned.\n\nListed worker artifact bytes are independently hashed in artifact-manifest.json. The primary report hash and input-integrity readback are in coordinator/final-readback.json. Budget observations are in coordinator/budget.json. Worker process evidence and qualitative findings remain bounded to their actual scope; static schema validation is not treated as native behavior.\n"

    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/measurement-method/outputs/worker/skills_how_references_explainer-prompt.md.txt",
      "bytes": 3411,
      "sha256": "08618881df380b5c74a030d6a577fd07f0fc6a3a91b4d0ec1795da3c6350c9b1",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-measurement-method",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Explainer Prompt Template\n\nBuild the explainer worker's prompt from this template. Fill in the placeholders.\n\n---\n\nYou are writing an architectural explanation for a senior engineer. For complex questions, explorers have traced different slices and gathered findings, independently when supported or sequentially with an explicit reduced-capability disclosure. For simple questions, omit Explorer Findings and explore the source yourself in this role. Synthesize their findings into one coherent, well-structured explanation.\n\n## Original Question\n\n> {QUESTION}\n\n## Explorer Findings\n\n{EXPLORER_FINDINGS_ALL}\n\n## Instructions\n\nThe explorers each investigated a different angle of the same subsystem. Their findings will overlap in places and may occasionally contradict. Reconcile them. Merge overlapping descriptions, resolve contradictions by checking the code yourself, and combine the separate slices into a unified picture.\n\nWrite an explanation a senior engineer unfamiliar with this area could read and walk away with a solid mental model, understanding the architecture well enough to start working in it confidently.\n\nYou have read-only access to the codebase to check anything, clarify a detail, or fill a gap. Use available file reading, symbol search and file discovery as needed. When explorer findings are supplied, you should not need to re-explore from scratch. Without them, trace entry points, calls, data, abstractions and boundaries yourself; disclose anything you cannot trace.\n\n## Output Format\n\nUse this structure, adapted to what makes sense for the question. Not every section is needed for every question.\n\n### Overview\n1-2 paragraphs. What is this thing, what does it do, why does it exist. Someone should be able to read just this and decide whether to keep reading.\n\n### Key Concepts\nThe important types, services, or abstractions needed to follow the rest. Brief definitions, not exhaustive.\n\n### How It Works\nThe core of the explanation, and the longest section. Walk through the flow: what triggers it, what happens step by step, where data goes, what the decision points are.\n\nUse prose, not pseudocode. Reference specific files and functions so the reader knows where to look, but don't dump large code blocks unless a snippet is essential to a point.\n\nWhen the flow involves multiple components talking to each other, or data transforming through stages, include a diagram. Use mermaid (```mermaid) for structured flows (sequence diagrams, flowcharts, component graphs) or ASCII art for simpler relationships where mermaid would be overkill. Use your judgment. A diagram should clarify, not decorate. If prose covers the flow, skip the diagram.\n\n### Where Things Live\nA brief file/directory map. Just the ones someone would need to start working here.\n\n### Gotchas\nNon-obvious things, surprising behavior, historical context, pitfalls. Skip this section if there's nothing worth calling out.\n\n## Communication Style\n\n- Use concrete language, not abstractions-about-abstractions\n- Say \"the `UserService` calls `AuthClient.refresh()`\" not \"the service delegates to the client\"\n- When something is complex, explain why it's complex. Don't just describe the complexity\n- When something is simple, don't pad it out\n- If there's a helpful analogy, use it. If there isn't, don't force one\n- If the explorers flagged open questions or gaps, acknowledge them rather than hiding them\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/measurement-method/outputs/worker/skills_poteto-mode_playbooks_investigation.md.txt",
      "bytes": 3104,
      "sha256": "f2172f39bc9b2365740b7ca932f6fc4b57e8b3269a4123f54b41d79d9c849360",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-measurement-method",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Investigation\n\nYou own the answer. Plan, route, write.\n\nInvestigation requests are read-only. They produce a cited explanation or a recommendation, not a code change. Read the complete [poteto mode](../SKILL.md) and this playbook before applying it. Preserve its Investigation routing for how, why, are-we-sure and choice-between-alternatives questions.\n\nBefore source reads or delegation, discover [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md) and follow [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md) and the [assignment contract](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Use only exposed, authorized read operations. Inherit parent model and effort unless a supported explicit selection is observed. Requested models are not observed models. The routed skills own their prompt and source contracts; this playbook directly invokes no script helper.\n\n1. Route through the [how skill](../../how/SKILL.md). For motivation questions, also route through the [why skill](../../why/SKILL.md). Read their complete instructions and the prompts and source playbooks required by the chosen route. Keep every investigator and explainer read-only, including connector-backed reads. Inspect delegated findings before relying on them. If independent workers are unavailable or unauthorized, disclose reduced capability and apply the same roles sequentially. Name inaccessible, denied, failed and searched-empty sources separately. Limit the answer to inspected evidence. Missing required companions or no authorized relevant source reads blocks the dependent answer.\n2. Throughput checkpoint stays one line: `throughput checkpoint: n/a, read-only investigation`.\n3. Produce the how-shaped output, Overview / Key Concepts / How It Works / Where Things Live / Gotchas, omitting sections only as how permits. For a decision between alternatives, produce a recommendation with a tradeoffs table. For motivation, preserve why confidence tiers, source coverage, contradictions and unknowns within the investigation output. Cite actual inspected sources. Accessible sources that support the answer are enough to deliver it without starting a product build or prototype.\n4. Apply the [unslop skill](../../unslop/SKILL.md) to the reply.\n\nNo PR, no babysit, no [architect](../../architect/SKILL.md) unless the investigation precedes a code change. If it does, hand back to the user and re-route to [Bug fix](bug-fix.md) or [Feature](feature.md). That handoff does not authorize implementation, commits, PR creation or other writes. The Investigation exception takes precedence over the mode index generic Opening a PR rule. Do not install tools, change credentials or modify source state to obtain evidence.\n\nReply with the investigation output. For are-we-sure answers, include your real judgment with reasons. Push back if the premise is wrong under the mode Autonomy rules. Broad autonomy never overrides user authorization or host permissions. Disclose unperformed reads, uncertain conclusions and reduced roles; structural checks establish no runtime behavior or independent-worker success.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/measurement-method/outputs/worker/references_model-roles.md.txt",
      "bytes": 2869,
      "sha256": "c86696b6c568b7db80a7e339856986e64482ce6a418bb91946ce3a3b772f79a1",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-measurement-method",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Model selection for portable roles\n\nUse [session capabilities](host-capabilities.md) before assigning a role. These [poteto](agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and [comment reviewer](agent-prompts/comment-sicko.md) prompts are instructions supplied to an exposed worker operation. Reading them does not register a custom agent or authorize delegation.\n\n| Role | Default requested model | Observed model | Supported effort | Fallback disclosure |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| poteto-agent | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\n| comment-sicko | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\n\nFor each assignment, record requested model and effort, observed model and effort, supported effort values and their source, worker identity, and fallback disclosure. Use `unknown` for information the operation does not expose. `inherit-parent` records a selection policy, not an observed model name. Do not silently convert a requested model into an actual model.\n\nSelect an explicit model only from current exposed tool documentation or observed enumeration results. If enumeration is missing, disclose that limit and retain `inherit-parent`. Documentation examples are not a list of available models. Use only an effort supported for the selected model by the current operation; do not carry an incompatible inherited effort into an explicit selection.\n\nIf a selected model or effort is rejected, retain the rejection. Use an authorized inherited or supported alternative only after disclosing the changed selection. If the task requires that exact selection, stop and record blocked. A fallback never proves the original selection succeeded.\n\nIdentical observed models mean no model diversity. Different roles can supply distinct instructions on the same model. Unknown observed models leave diversity unverified, even when requested model names differ. Different observed models support a model-diversity statement only for those observed assignments. Worker independence still requires successful spawn and result inspection.\n\nWhen workers are unavailable, apply an authorized role sequentially and label reduced capability. Preserve scope and permission boundaries. Do not claim independent review, background execution, or full worker support from sequential role switching.\n\nThe cached documentation from https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/subagents describes inheritance and optional local agent configuration. It is context only. This package writes no local custom-agent configuration and claims no current host model or worker support from that documentation.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/measurement-method/outputs/worker/result.md",
      "bytes": 10543,
      "sha256": "0ab89408004570a319282584d2732b9e24cebe9e31b3ee78da6ebc3607713fab",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-measurement-method",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Inconclusive\n\nRevision B has no verified export-performance improvement over revision A in the supplied evidence. The prompt says one run looks 30% faster. It supplies no durations, units, raw samples, exact compared revisions, error counts, or proof that writes finished. I observed zero benchmark runs and zero profiling runs. No adoption recommendation follows from this evidence.\n\nThis is an assessment of the supplied claim and the measurement method. The A/B experiment is blocked by missing inputs. Authorized inspection and evidence persistence succeeded. I did not run an unrelated shared scenario to manufacture a comparison.\n\n## Assignment and inspected scope\n\n- Assignment is `measurement-method-native-1`. Output owner is `/root/measurement_method`.\n- Supplied input revision and observed case HEAD both equal `aaf87c83b4eff916bcc8b95cf288169691382fb3`. This identifies the input repository. It does not identify either export implementation A or B.\n- Supplied sourceFreeze is `51f209daf83cd97d64f89881d66533bb59dc57db`. The instruction source is the frozen package at `/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3`. Its inspected `plugin.json` declares version `0.1.3`.\n- Requested model and effort are `inherit-parent`. Observed model and effort are `unknown`. No runtime observation provided them.\n- The supplied `fixtures` array is empty. The root inventory and committed file names show shared synthetic scenarios. None is selected for this assignment. I inspected their inventory and shared README, without reading or executing unrelated scenario data.\n- No case `AGENTS.md` exists. The provided instructions govern the case. No source files or Git state were changed. Writes are confined to `outputs/worker`.\n\n## Measurement record\n\n| Field | Evidence and limit |\n| --- | --- |\n| Intended claim | Test whether B reduces completed export time relative to A for the requested 60,000-row workload. The current 30% statement is unverified. |\n| Workload | Export, per the prompt. Schema, row contents, destination, bytes, and expected output are absent. |\n| Dataset size | 60,000 rows requested. No dataset or completed row count was observed. |\n| Concurrency | Unknown. |\n| Timed region | Unknown. No measurement script is selected or supplied. |\n| Exact A and B revisions | Both unknown. The input repository HEAD is not a substitute. |\n| Configurations | Runtime, dependencies, build mode, environment, database, batching, transactions, pools, and cache policy are unknown. |\n| Samples | One apparent run is asserted in the prompt. Per-side sample allocation is unknown. Zero raw samples were inspected and zero benchmark samples were executed. |\n| Duration units, median, range | Unavailable. No milliseconds, seconds, throughput, median, or range can be reported. |\n| Limiter | Unknown. No separate profile or counters from an export run exist. |\n\n## Capabilities and machine context\n\nThe exposed execution binding is `functions.exec` with `tools.exec_command`. Read-only commands launched through Bun succeeded. The same binding read exact source files and wrote isolated evidence. Persistence was checked by reading saved artifacts back. The scoped authorization permits inspection, execution, and writes under `outputs/worker` and `outputs/working`. It forbids installs, external actions, Git mutations, and additional workers.\n\n`capability-probes.json` retains executable discovery, exact command arguments, stdout, stderr, and exit codes. `uptime` reported load averages `14.54 9.00 7.41` during the first probe. `sysctl -n hw.logicalcpu` failed with exit 1 and `Operation not permitted`. Bun's `node:os.cpus()` returned 14 logical CPUs. That API result does not establish allocated CPU quota or prove an idle machine. The load relative to exposed logical CPUs warrants contention checks before future measurements, but it does not identify contention during the unobserved run. No process was stopped.\n\nNo export runner, selected dataset, A/B configuration, output artifact, or benchmark profile is available in this assigned scope. Available system commands cannot replace those inputs. No profiler was installed or launched, and no synthetic timing loop was used as a substitute. The 12-minute ceiling was stated before inspection. No costly experiment was started.\n\n## All seven questions\n\n| Canonical question | Current finding | Required method |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| 1. Why not double? | Unverified. There is no evidenced limiter. | Run a separate profile for the real export and map the hot path or resource counter to source. Check CPU, I/O, locks, and the load generator. Exclude profiling runs from reported timing samples. |\n| 2. Was it tuned? | Unknown for both sides. | Use each side's production build and settings. Record runtime and dependency versions, batching, transactions, pools, indexes, and cache policy. Tune an avoidable configuration limiter and rerun before choosing an option. If tuning is unavailable, withhold an adoption winner. |\n| 3. Did it break limits? | Cannot calculate a physical bound without elapsed time, bytes, bandwidth, CPU quota, or changed-path share. | Compute bytes divided by seconds against disk and network limits. Compare operations per second times CPU cost per operation against available core-seconds. Check the maximum saving permitted by the changed component's end-to-end share. A number beyond those bounds requires investigation of skipped work, caching, or bugs. |\n| 4. Did it error? | Explicitly unknown. No error counter or correct-output check was supplied. | Record attempted, successful, rejected, failed, timed-out, and retried work for every sample. Verify exact expected row counts and content against an independent expected result. Output existence alone is insufficient. Add missing error counters to the actual runner within authorized scope. |\n| 5. Does it reproduce? | No inspected raw samples. A single asserted run cannot establish repeatability. | After a declared warmup policy, run at least five unprofiled samples per side in alternating order, A1, B1, A2, B2, through A5, B5. Keep the same dataset, concurrency, machine, configuration, and cache policy. Report every sample, per-side median and min-to-max range. Treat a gap below run-to-run variation as no measurable difference. Use a rank-sum test or supported runner statistics for a close call. |\n| 6. Does it matter? | Unknown whether the assertion times an entire user-visible export or a helper. | Measure the full path the user waits for with realistic data and concurrency. Include queueing and completion where relevant. Report any micro result as a share of that full duration. |\n| 7. Did it even happen? | Explicitly unknown whether writes finished or occurred within the timed region. | Start timing before the intended export work and stop only after awaited processing, required commits, stream completion, and destination acknowledgment. Confirm the requested rows and bytes were written, and that the result was consumed. State whether durability or only acknowledgment defines completion. |\n\n## How the comparison would run\n\nFirst supply the exact A and B commit identifiers, runnable checkout locations, dependency versions, production configurations, dataset, expected output, concurrency, destination, and a measurement script. Read the script before executing it. Record what it times, counts, and ignores. Define completion and keep every write or await required by that definition inside the timed region. Confirm correctness and error counters on both sides before performance interpretation.\n\nRecord machine contention and CPU allocation. Use a declared warmup and cache policy matching production. Obtain the limiter from a separate profiling run. Then perform at least ten timing samples in alternating order, five for A and five for B. Capture raw durations in an explicit unit together with correctness, error, and completed-work counts for every sample. Keep profile overhead out of those samples.\n\nCalculate medians and ranges, compare the gap with observed variation, check physical limits, and measure the end-to-end export path if the original script only times part of it. Report faster, slower, no measurable difference, or inconclusive with the unit, sample count, range, and evidenced limiter. Missing limiter, untuned sides, unchecked errors, or unverified completed work keep an improvement claim inconclusive.\n\nThe one-run exception applies only when the user explicitly requests a quick ballpark. That report must say it is one run and still verify questions 4 and 7. It cannot choose between adoption options. This prompt requests an A/B improvement assessment and does not invoke that exception. Even a requested ballpark would remain unsupported here because errors and completed writes are unknown.\n\n## Where the evidence lives\n\nAll paths below are relative to this `outputs/worker` directory.\n\n- `inputs.json` contains full supplied assignment, input, and task contract texts with SHA-256 hashes.\n- `rev-parse.json`, `status.json`, and `ls-tree.json` contain initial exact Git command arguments and results.\n- `raw-command-records.json` contains a repeated revision check, read-only inventory, status, and uptime with exact arguments and unabridged stdout, stderr, and exit codes.\n- `capability-probes.json` contains discovery and the failed CPU-count command as well as successful probes.\n- `inspection.json` records the root inventory, absent selected fixtures, missing runner and A/B inputs, and Bun OS CPU observation.\n- `source-reads.json` contains the exact workflow source paths, hashes, and inspected root documentation.\n- `source-snapshot-hashes.json` hashes every saved instruction snapshot. The `.txt` files retain full source bytes, including mode, Principles index, benchmark checklist, applied principle leaves, and linked support.\n\nExplain the Number changed the decision to withhold the 30% improvement claim. Prove It Works changed the evidence handling to inspect actual revisions and read saved artifacts back. Unslop and Technical writing shaped this report. The simple explanation role was performed directly because additional workers were forbidden. No independent review is claimed. Source metadata `disable-model-invocation: true` was preserved in snapshots without claiming the host enforces it.\n\nNo host pass or grading conclusion is claimed by this report. The coordinator owns qualitative review and integration.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/measurement-method/outputs/worker/plugin.json.txt",
      "bytes": 749,
      "sha256": "6a4d33e5a6a9f8974f31b4233dcc0b9fa558599246436dd9a4a3aa8b4567f174",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-measurement-method",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\n  \"$schema\": \"https://agent-plugins.org/schemas/1.0.0/plugin.schema.json\",\n  \"name\": \"pstack-openai\",\n  \"version\": \"0.1.3\",\n  \"description\": \"Unofficial port of pstack engineering workflows and principles.\",\n  \"license\": \"MIT\",\n  \"extensions\": {\n    \"com.openai\": {\n      \"interface\": {\n        \"displayName\": \"pstack-openai\",\n        \"shortDescription\": \"pstack engineering workflows\",\n        \"logo\": \"./assets/logo.png\",\n        \"websiteURL\": \"https://github.com/cursor/plugins/blob/main/pstack/docs/guide/README.md\",\n        \"developerName\": \"Lauren Tan\"\n      }\n    }\n  },\n  \"homepage\": \"https://github.com/cursor/plugins/blob/main/pstack/docs/guide/README.md\",\n  \"author\": {\n    \"name\": \"Lauren Tan\",\n    \"url\": \"https://x.com/poteto\"\n  }\n}\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/measurement-method/outputs/worker/references_workflow-ownership.md.txt",
      "bytes": 1265,
      "sha256": "50644b3a9a3dfd5651382eaca1d3a2728af0b3157d6d66b730930370a64bb03c",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-measurement-method",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Workflow ownership\n\nAn explicit pstack invocation gives pstack ownership of that workflow. An explicit Superpowers invocation gives Superpowers ownership. Do not run both controllers over the same assignment or let one silently replace the other's plan, review, or integration decisions.\n\nFor an unqualified request, D1 retains the already active workflow. With no active workflow, use host defaults and do not activate either orchestration mode automatically. If both pstack and Superpowers are explicitly requested, clarify ownership before launching workers or changing artifacts. Preserve the active controller across follow-ups unless the user changes ownership. A helper skill can advise the owner without becoming a second controller.\n\nThe owner assigns one coordinator. Workers produce isolated artifacts and evidence; the coordinator reviews and integrates them. Shared inventory and registry writes belong to the coordinator. Worker cancellation, stale results or unavailable tools do not transfer that ownership automatically.\n\nHost permissions and user authorization constrain every owner. Local structural tests, documented APIs and declared cases provide no host pass. Explicit reduced or blocked results remain visible beside the full expectation.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/measurement-method/outputs/worker/skills_how_SKILL.md.txt",
      "bytes": 4092,
      "sha256": "bca7b43af6d6d0089d20edb2ec2178d681d589357c20ea345eddbf81ce87f7e8",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-measurement-method",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: how\ndescription: \"Use for \\\"how does X work\\\", code walkthroughs before changing something, and placement / ownership / layering questions (\\\"where should this live\\\", \\\"which package owns this\\\", \\\"is this the right layer\\\"). Explains subsystem architecture, runtime flow, onboarding mental models. Use why for motivation.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# How\n\nExplore the codebase to answer \"how does X work?\" questions. Produce architectural explanations at the level of a senior engineer onboarding onto a subsystem, enough to build a working mental model, not so much that it reads like annotated source code.\n\nBefore dispatch, read [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Bind worker operations to tools actually exposed and authorized in this session. Request the inherited parent model and effort unless a supported explicit selection is observed. Retain rejections and disclose replacements; an unavailable required selection is blocked. Record requested and observed selections separately, using unknown for unobserved values.\n\nKeep explorers and explainers read-only. Worker assignments record their role, distinct angle, exact input revision, identity and inspected results under the assignment contract. Reading a prompt does not register a worker. If independent workers are unavailable or unauthorized, disclose reduced capability and perform the same exploration and explanation roles sequentially. Never claim parallel or independent exploration from sequential work. If source access is unavailable or denied, report the gap and limit the explanation to inspected evidence.\n\n## Step 1. Assess Complexity\n\nIf the scope is ambiguous, state your interpretation and explore. The user can redirect.\n\n- **Simple** (a single module, a small utility, a narrow question such as \"how does function X work\"): no explorers. One explainer explores and explains in a single pass. Go to Step 2b.\n- **Complex** (a subsystem spanning multiple files or services, a cross-cutting feature, a full architectural overview): use parallel explorers when available, then hand off to the explainer. Go to Step 2a.\n\nWhen in doubt, take the simple path.\n\n## Step 2a. Explore (complex questions only)\n\nDecompose the question into 2 to 4 exploration angles, each a distinct slice of the subsystem. Dispatch all explorers together through an observed worker binding, with distinct assignments:\n\n- Role: how explorer\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\n- Access: read-only\n\nEach explorer gets the prompt in `references/explorer-prompt.md` with its angle filled in. If workers are unavailable, explore those distinct angles sequentially and retain separate findings. Then go to Step 3.\n\n## Step 2b. Direct Explain (simple questions)\n\nAssign one explainer worker that explores and explains in one pass:\n\n- Role: how explainer\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\n- Access: read-only\n\nBuild its prompt from `references/explainer-prompt.md` without the explorer-findings section. The explainer reads and traces the source itself. If workers are unavailable, apply this role sequentially. Go to Step 4.\n\n## Step 3. Synthesize (complex questions only)\n\nOnce all explorer results have been inspected, assign one explainer worker to synthesize their findings into one explanation:\n\n- Role: how explainer\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\n- Access: read-only\n\nBuild its prompt from `references/explainer-prompt.md` with every explorer's findings filled in. In reduced capability, synthesize the sequential findings under the same read-only role.\n\n## Step 4. Present\n\nPresent the explainer's output to the user. Light edits for clarity or context from the conversation are fine. Do not substantially rewrite it.\n\n## Output Format\n\nThe explanation uses the sections defined in `references/explainer-prompt.md`, dropping any that do not apply: Overview, Key Concepts, How It Works, Where Things Live, Gotchas.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/measurement-method/outputs/worker/skills_benchmark-checklist_SKILL.md.txt",
      "bytes": 6476,
      "sha256": "81ecaa398ff10882a1e1050c529c04c3a678eecc03e36ad24549379c3776d106",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-measurement-method",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: benchmark-checklist\ndescription: \"Vet a perf measurement (limiter, tuning, limits, errors, repeatability, relevance, and whether the work happened) before you report or act on it. Use when you run a benchmark or report a speedup or regression you measured.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Benchmark checklist\n\nUse this when you produce a performance number: a PR's before and after, a regression claim, a hillclimb harness, or a library or config choice. [Explain the Number](../principle-explain-the-number/SKILL.md) says why. Answer each question below with evidence from a run, not from a guess about the code.\n\nFor a quick ballpark the user asked for, one run is enough. Still check questions 4 and 7, and say that it is one run. Skip the rest unless that run looks wrong. A choice between options is never a ballpark.\n\n## Capability and permission checks\n\nUse [host-capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to check available inspection, execution, profiling and evidence storage before a run. Use commands only when exposed and authorized. The commands below are examples for compatible systems; use an available platform equivalent when a command is missing. Do not install profilers or stop unrelated processes without authorization. If a check is unavailable, name it and retain the report gates below. Reviewing supplied artifacts can produce a reduced assessment, but cannot supply missing run evidence. If neither artifacts nor authorized inspection are available, report the measurement as blocked.\n\nRecord the workload, data size, concurrency, timed region, exact revisions and configurations of every side, and sample count with the run evidence. Check machine contention, tuning, caches, warmup, errors and completed work before claiming a measured improvement. Preserve `disable-model-invocation: true` as source metadata without assuming the host enforces it.\n\n## Before you run anything\n\n- Write down the claim you expect to make, in the words you would ship (\"export is 30% faster at p50 on the 60k-row dataset\"). The questions test that sentence.\n- Read the measurement script. Note what it times, what it counts, and what it ignores.\n- Check the load average with `uptime` and the core count with `nproc`. If the machine is busy, find out what is running. If you cannot stop it, interleave the sides so both see the same noise, and say so in the report.\n\n## The questions\n\n1. **Why not double?** Name the limiter. Profile in a run you do not report, because profilers and tracers slow the work down. Use CPU per process (`top`, `pidstat`), a profiler for the runtime (`node --cpu-prof`, `py-spy`, `perf`), I/O wait, and syscall counts (`strace -c` on Linux). Then map the hot spot to source. Watch the load generator too. If it saturates first, you measured the load generator. If a change did not move the number, the limiter explains why, so find it before you call the change useless.\n2. **Was it tuned?** Run every side the way production runs it: release builds, production flags and env, batching and transaction settings, connection pools, caches as warm or cold as production sees them, and the same versions and data. If one side runs on defaults, you compared configurations, not implementations. A limiter that is a setting, such as a commit per row, a debug build, or a missing index, means that side is untuned. Tune it and measure again before you pick a winner. If you cannot tune it, do not pick a winner from that run. Narrowing the claim to the code as it ships today does not fix this when the user is choosing what to adopt, because they adopt the option, not today's settings.\n3. **Did it break limits?** Do the arithmetic. Compare bytes per second with disk and network bandwidth, and operations per second times the cost per operation with the cores you have. Compare the time saved with the time the changed piece took. Removing a piece that takes 10% of the run can make the run at most about 11% faster. A result past a limit means the run measured something other than the work, such as a cache, a no-op, or a bug.\n4. **Did it error?** Count failures and non-success responses, and check that the outputs are correct, not just present. Errors behave differently from successes. Rejections are often fast, and timeouts and retries are slow. If the script does not count errors, add the count.\n5. **Does it reproduce?** Run each side at least 5 times, and alternate the sides (A, B, A, B, and so on) so that warmup, lazy initialization, caches, and drift do not favor one side. Report the median and the range. Treat a gap smaller than the run-to-run variation as no measurable difference. When the call is close, use a rank-sum test or the harness's own statistics.\n6. **Does it matter?** Next to any micro result, measure the end-to-end path a user waits on, with realistic data sizes and concurrency. Report the micro result as a share of the whole. A helper that takes 1% of a request can make the request at most 1% faster, however fast the helper gets.\n7. **Did it even happen?** Confirm the work ran inside the timed region. The request reached the server, the rows were written, the bytes were read, and the code used the result. Lazy code (generators nobody iterates, promises nobody awaits, results the JIT can discard) and timeouts all produce numbers for work that never happened.\n\n## Report\n\n- Lead with the verdict: faster, slower, no measurable difference, or inconclusive.\n- Give the number with its unit, the run count, the range, and the limiter. For example, \"p50 41 ms → 33 ms, median of 7 runs per side, range 32 to 35 ms after, bound by JSON parsing on one core.\"\n- Call the verdict inconclusive when you claim a difference but cannot name the limiter, when a side ran untuned, or when you could not check questions 4 and 7. Name the gap.\n- Keep a PR body to one primary number, per the **Opening a PR** playbook. Put the runs, the range, and the limiter evidence in a linked artifact or a notes file.\n\n## How this fits the other perf material\n\n- The **Perf issue** playbook finds and fixes slowness, and the performance mantras in its step 2 generate the fixes. This skill vets its baseline before the playbook plans from it, and every number after that.\n- The **Hillclimb** playbook loops on one metric. This skill vets its harness before the harness is frozen. The frozen harness then prints error and work counts, so each keep-or-revert checks questions 4 and 7 for free.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/measurement-method/outputs/worker/references_host-capabilities.md.txt",
      "bytes": 7155,
      "sha256": "f0f09ce28b6feca4d72694d5687204dc074aa58dade9a0ab59656da3bfa1bd20",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-measurement-method",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Discover capabilities in the current session\n\nRead the tools exposed to this session before assigning work. Cached official-subagents.txt is documentation context; it proves no operation succeeded. Local agent configuration is not portable plugin configuration.\n\nRecord each binding as `{operation, tool, exposed, succeeded, evidence, limits}`. `tool` is the exact exposed identifier or null when absent. `succeeded` is true only after observing a result. Keep failed and unavailable bindings. Successful bindings become `successfulOperations` in host evidence, with a root-relative evidence file. The operation labels below describe purposes, never callable APIs.\n\n| Operation | Discovery and observation required |\n| --- | --- |\n| spawn | Find an exposed independent-worker operation; retain returned worker identity and exact assigned revision. |\n| follow-up | Find the operation that sends input to that worker; observe acceptance by the same identity. |\n| wait | Find the operation that waits for worker state; retain terminal or interrupted state. |\n| inspect-result | Read the worker result and its referenced artifacts; a wait notification alone is insufficient. |\n| stop | Observe cancellation or termination through the exposed operation. |\n| read | Observe the requested file content through the available file tool. |\n| isolated-write | Observe a write within the worker's assigned path and permissions. |\n| history | Observe available prior turns or records; declare gaps explicitly. |\n| execution | Observe command execution with exit status and output. |\n| persistence | Write and read an authorized durable record; a claimed save is insufficient. |\n| external-write | Record explicit authorization and action identity; inspect the result before replay. |\n| resolve-ownership | Observe distinct owned paths before contested writes. |\n| inspect-terminal | Observe the worker's terminal state after stop. |\n| quarantine-artifacts | Observe partial artifacts withheld from integration. |\n| inspect-prior-action | Read the interrupted action identity and prior outcome. |\n| inspect-destination | Observe the destination state for that action identity. |\n| safe-recovery | Observe explicitly authorized idempotent recovery after identity and destination inspection. |\n\nNo default API names are assigned here. Tool names differ by host and session. An exposed tool may still be denied or fail. Record requested and actual models only when observed; otherwise use `unknown`. A requested model never proves the actual model.\n\nA denied or unavailable operation blocks the branch that requires it. Sequential substitution for required independent work is reduced capability and uses `reduced-pass`; ordinary direct completion can fully pass. Missing persistence limits session pickup even when current work succeeds. Preserve user authorization and host permission boundaries through every fallback.\n\nWorker passes require observed spawn and result inspection. Each registry entry declares `requiredObservations` for `pass` and `reduced-pass`; tool observations need exposed bindings and existing evidence artifacts; native text observations use the separate reviewed native record below. Full cancellation requires stop, terminal inspection and artifact quarantine. Full persistence requires durable write and readback. Full interrupted-action recovery requires prior identity inspection, destination inspection, safe recovery, explicit external permission and a recorded prior action identity even when no external-write observation is listed.\n\nPermission checks follow performed operations. Isolated writes and persistence require granted writes. External writes and safe recovery require granted external permission and prior identity. Authorized read-only reductions may retain denied writes; their limits must preserve denied or unavailable permissions. Required observations remain applicable to those reductions.\n\nHost evidence is stored as object or array JSON files under `docs/parity/evidence/`. Required fields are defined by `docs/parity/evidence.schema.json`. Artifacts and operation evidence must be existing files inside the tested root. Test fixtures belong in `tests/` and prove validator behavior only.\n\nRun `python3 tools/validate_evidence.py --root . --commit <tested-package-commit>` through Bun, for example `bun -e 'const p=Bun.spawnSync([\"python3\",\"-B\",\"tools/validate_evidence.py\",\"--root\",\".\",\"--commit\",process.argv[1]],{env:{...process.env,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE:\"1\"}});process.stdout.write(p.stdout);process.stderr.write(p.stderr);process.exit(p.exitCode)' <tested-package-commit>`. It reads every JSON record below the evidence directory, prints each error, and exits 1 for invalid records or 0 for valid records. An empty directory has no invalid records and supplies no behavioral evidence.\n\nThe required registry file in `docs/parity/case-registry.json` declares applicability. Missing or non-file registry, unknown case, structural-only entry or unlisted host rejects behavioral validation. Maintenance fixtures use a temporary copy of this registry; they cannot bypass applicability. Required hosts must receive observed evidence or explicit blocked records before release claims. Structural checks have no required host and cannot populate host status. Later producers propose task-owned entries; only the coordinator integrates them. Source paths select callers through the task 3 case contract. Case definitions alone leave host statuses `not-run`.\n\n## Direct completion and recorded observations\n\n`direct` means the task is completed in the current context. Restatement, supplied-text writing and complete installed discovery can pass directly. `sequential` is reserved for replacing a required independent method with sequential work and remains reduced. A text response has no invented callable tool. Record supplied input, the actual response and qualitative outcome review as `nativeObservations`, each with its existing listed evidence artifact and named reviewer. Their labels are `supplied-input`, `response` and `review-outcome`. The reviewer assesses the case's expected outcome; validation checks evidence relationships and does not decide prose quality.\n\nEach registry entry declares allowed execution modes and required permissions for each status. A grant is required for the performed action: worker spawn, command execution and stop need execution; isolated output or persistence needs writes; external actions need their separate permission and prior identity. Read-only discovery does not inherit an execution grant requirement. Tool observations still name an actually exposed tool and existing evidence. Native observations cannot claim tool discovery, worker identity or saved writes.\n\nFull Arena and Swarm results additionally provide `inputRevision` and a listed `methodReceipt` artifact. An exposed `inspect-method` operation must reference that receipt. Assignment operations name `assignmentId` and the corresponding inspected output. See the [structured method contract](orchestration-contract.md). All positive maintenance fixtures are synthetic; none is an observed host result.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/measurement-method/outputs/worker/skills_principle-prove-it-works_SKILL.md.txt",
      "bytes": 1814,
      "sha256": "a78cfdebd9dccfba5f6d69bbd74379924769d769e4b3e2f514846fc7bdd56edc",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-measurement-method",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: principle-prove-it-works\ndescription: \"Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, self-report, or 'it compiles.'\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Prove It Works\n\nVerify every task output by checking the real thing directly. Do not infer from proxies, self-reports, or \"it compiles.\"\n\n**Why:** Unverified work has unknown correctness. Indirect verification (file mtimes, output freshness, agent self-reports, cached screenshots) feels cheaper than direct observation. Acting on a wrong inference costs far more than checking the source.\n\nCheck the real thing, not a proxy:\n- Check process liveness directly, not indirectly through derived state\n- Read the actual value, not a cached or derived representation\n- When verification fails, suspect the observation method before suspecting the system\n\n## Script the check when you can\n\nThe strongest proof is a deterministic script that re-runs the same comparison, not a one-time eyeball. Write the script, run it, and keep its output as an artifact a reviewer can re-run instead of trusting your word.\n\nKeep the artifact visible for the human. Commit it only for large or complex work where the trail has to be auditable later, like a big port or migration (the **show-me-your-work** skill).\n\n## Session capabilities and permissions\n\nUse the exposed tools and observed operations described in [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md). Run checks and retain artifacts only within the user's authorized scope and host permissions. If direct inspection or execution is unavailable or denied, state which outputs remain unverified and withhold completion for those outputs. A proposed script is not evidence that it ran.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/measurement-method/outputs/worker/capability-probes.json",
      "bytes": 1738,
      "sha256": "a6b73a208a4bfedc58db3f480174f4238b8946e3df782b94de28cfc9efd10581",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-measurement-method",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\n  \"availability\": {\n    \"bun\": \"/opt/homebrew/bin/bun\",\n    \"git\": \"/opt/homebrew/bin/git\",\n    \"uptime\": \"/usr/bin/uptime\",\n    \"nproc\": null,\n    \"sysctl\": \"/usr/sbin/sysctl\",\n    \"top\": \"/usr/bin/top\",\n    \"pidstat\": null,\n    \"perf\": null,\n    \"strace\": null,\n    \"py-spy\": null\n  },\n  \"runs\": [\n    {\n      \"args\": [\n        \"uptime\"\n      ],\n      \"cwd\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/measurement-method\",\n      \"stdout\": \"22:07  1 user, load averages: 14.54 9.00 7.41\\n\",\n      \"stderr\": \"\",\n      \"exitCode\": 0\n    },\n    {\n      \"args\": [\n        \"sysctl\",\n        \"-n\",\n        \"hw.logicalcpu\"\n      ],\n      \"cwd\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/measurement-method\",\n      \"stdout\": \"\",\n      \"stderr\": \"sysctl: sysctl fmt -1 1024 1: Operation not permitted\\n\",\n      \"exitCode\": 1\n    },\n    {\n      \"args\": [\n        \"git\",\n        \"rev-parse\",\n        \"HEAD\"\n      ],\n      \"cwd\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/measurement-method\",\n      \"stdout\": \"aaf87c83b4eff916bcc8b95cf288169691382fb3\\n\",\n      \"stderr\": \"\",\n      \"exitCode\": 0\n    }\n  ],\n  \"permissions\": {\n    \"inspection\": true,\n    \"execution\": true,\n    \"write\": [\n      \"outputs/worker\",\n      \"outputs/working\"\n    ],\n    \"profiles\": \"Only authorized scoped benchmark. No runner or revisions supplied.\",\n    \"installs\": false,\n    \"workers\": false,\n    \"externalActions\": false\n  },\n  \"binding\": {\n    \"operation\": \"execution\",\n    \"tool\": \"functions.exec -> tools.exec_command\",\n    \"exposed\": true,\n    \"succeeded\": true,\n    \"evidence\": \"capability-probes.json\",\n    \"limits\": \"Commands launched through Bun; no benchmark executed\"\n  }\n}"

    {

      "text": "{\n  \"outputs/assignment.json\": {\n    \"text\": \"{\\n  \\\"id\\\": \\\"measurement-method-native-1\\\",\\n  \\\"scope\\\": {\\n    \\\"caseId\\\": \\\"measurement-method\\\",\\n    \\\"cwd\\\": \\\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/measurement-method\\\",\\n    \\\"inputRevision\\\": \\\"aaf87c83b4eff916bcc8b95cf288169691382fb3\\\",\\n \\\"caseFile\\\": \\\"docs/parity/cases/skills/benchmark-checklist.json\\\",\\n        \\\"sourcePaths\\\": [\\n          \\\"skills/benchmark-checklist/SKILL.md\\\"\\n        ],\\n \\\"not-run\\\",\\n          \\\"codex\\\": \\\"not-run\\\"\\n        },\\n        \\\"requiredObservations\\\": {\\n          \\\"pass\\\": [\\n            \\\"read\\\",\\n            \\\"review-outcome\\\",\\n            \\\"execution\\\"\\n          ],\\n          \\\"reduced-pass\\\": [\\n            \\\"read\\\"\\n          ]\\n        },\\n        \\\"executionModes\\\": {\\n       "owner": "local-013-initial-measurement-method",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Workflow checklist\n\n- [x] Read required full mode and Principles index, exact benchmark-checklist source, and applied leaves.\n- [x] Match immutable assignment, canonical cwd, and full input revision.\n- [x] Inspect exposed tools, scope and permissions.\n- [x] Inspect selected benchmark inputs and available artifacts.\n- [x] Explain method and apply all seven canonical questions.\n- [x] Preserve requested-ballpark exception without applying it to this comparison.\n- [x] Persist evidence and verify actual output.\n- [ ] Execute A/B benchmark variants and separate profiling run. Blocked because revisions, runner, dataset, settings and destination are absent.\n\nthroughput checkpoint: n/a, read-only investigation\n\nThe case is a simple measurement-method explanation. Additional workers are forbidden. Investigation roles were performed directly with reduced independent-worker capability. No source edits, PR or implementation work applies.\n\nThe Investigation steps are retained verbatim below. This local checklist was persisted after inspection rather than before it. That chronology is not changed by these checkmarks.\n\n1. Route through the [how skill](../../how/SKILL.md). For motivation questions, also route through the [why skill](../../why/SKILL.md). Read their complete instructions and the prompts and source playbooks required by the chosen route. Keep every investigator and explainer read-only, including connector-backed reads. Inspect delegated findings before relying on them. If independent workers are unavailable or unauthorized, disclose reduced capability and apply the same roles sequentially. Name inaccessible, denied, failed and searched-empty sources separately. Limit the answer to inspected evidence. Missing required companions or no authorized relevant source reads blocks the dependent answer.\n2. Throughput checkpoint stays one line: `throughput checkpoint: n/a, read-only investigation`.\n3. Produce the how-shaped output, Overview / Key Concepts / How It Works / Where Things Live / Gotchas, omitting sections only as how permits. For a decision between alternatives, produce a recommendation with a tradeoffs table. For motivation, preserve why confidence tiers, source coverage, contradictions and unknowns within the investigation output. Cite actual inspected sources. Accessible sources that support the answer are enough to deliver it without starting a product build or prototype.\n4. Apply the [unslop skill](../../unslop/SKILL.md) to the reply.\n"
