Overview\\n\\n`cents(value)` returns an integer input unchanged and throws for other inputs. `legacyClient(api)` returns `api.retryAfter`. Both are exported entry points in [source.mjs](../../committed-inputs/shared/additional/legal-header/source.mjs).\\n\\nThe source does not prove that a caller already applied tax rounding or that the `@ts-expect-error` is correct. Those comments make claims whose supporting caller, upstream contract, and compiler context are absent from the two inspected files.\\n\\n## How it works\\n\\nA caller invokes `cents(value)`. The function passes `value` to `Number.isInteger` on line 5. A false result throws `Error` with message `fractional cents`. A true result reaches line 7 and returns the original `value`. There is no conversion, tax calculation, or rounding operation. The public-contract comment on line 2 matches this control flow.\\n\\nThe `IMPORTANT` comment on line 6 says the caller already applied the tax rounding boundary and that another round would lose reconciliation. The implementation preserves an accepted input, which is consistent with avoiding another round. Neither file includes a caller, rounding rule, or reconciliation calculation, so the claimed requirement and its consequence remain unverified.\\n\\nA caller invokes `legacyClient(api)`. Line 11 accesses `api.retryAfter` and returns that property value. The function performs no explicit validation and never calls the property. A missing property on an ordinary object yields `undefined`. A null or undefined `api` causes the property access to throw.\\n\\nThe comment on line 10 places `@ts-expect-error` directly before that property access. Its stated reason is that an upstream fixture client lacks `retryAfter` until pinned client contract v2. The comment has no runtime effect. No upstream client declaration, version pin, type-check configuration, or diagnostic is present in the inspected source or diff. Consequently, the files establish where the suppression sits and what its author claims, but cannot establish whether a checker processes it, whether the next line has the expected error, or whether suppressing that error is justified.\\n\\n## Where things live\\n\\n- [source.mjs](../../committed-inputs/shared/additional/legal-header/source.mjs) contains the MIT SPDX header on line 1, `cents` on lines 3 through 8, and `legacyClient` on lines 9 through 12.\\n- [source.diff](../../committed-inputs/shared/additional/legal-header/source.diff) presents that same source as a new file. Its added lines exactly match the inspected source. It supplies no caller or earlier implementation.\\n\\n## Gotchas\\n\\nThe error text says `fractional cents`, but the actual rejection rule is any input for which `Number.isInteger` returns false. The guard does not enforce a tax policy or prove that accepted input was rounded upstream.\\n\\nTreat the caller-rounding assertion and the upstream-client assertion as unverified context. Establishing either requires evidence beyond this authorized two-file scope. The SPDX header is source metadata and does not alter either function.\\n\\n\",\"exit_code\":0,\"status\":\"completed\"}}\n{\"type\":\"item.started\",\"item\":{\"id\":\"item_44\",\"type\":\"file_change\",\"changes\":[{\"path\":\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/codex/batch-06/legal-negative.ts\",\"kind\":\"add\"}],\"status\":\"in_progress\"}}\n{\"type\":\"item.completed\",\"item\":{\"id\":\"item_44\",\"type\":\"file_change\",\"changes\":[{\"path\":\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/codex/batch-06/legal-negative.ts\",\"kind\":\"add\"}],\"status\":\"completed\"}}\n{\"type\":\"item.started\",\"item\":{\"id\":\"item_45\",\"type\":\"command_execution\",\"command\":\"/bin/zsh -lc 'bun run legal-negative.ts'\",\"aggregated_output\":\"\",\"exit_code\":null,\"status\":\"in_progress\"}}\n{\"type\":\"item.completed\",\"item\":{\"id\":\"item_45\",\"type\":\"command_execution\",\"command\":\"/bin/zsh -lc 'bun run legal-negative.ts'\",\"aggregated_output\":\"{\\\"target\\\":\\\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/legal-header/outputs/negative-missing-workers/source.mjs\\\",\\\"deletedComments\\\":2,\\\"diffExitCode\\\":1}\\n\",\"exit_code\":0,\"status\":\"completed\"}}\n{\"type\":\"item.started\",\"item\":{\"id\":\"item_46\",\"type\":\"file_change\",\"changes\":[{\"path\":\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/legal-header/outputs/coordinator/review.md\",\"kind\":\"add\"}],\"status\":\"in_progress\"}}\n{\"type\":\"item.completed\",\"item\":{\"id\":\"item_46\",\"type\":\"file_change\",\"changes\":[{\"path\":\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/legal-header/outputs/coordinator/review.md\",\"kind\":\"add\"}],\"status\":\"completed\"}}\n{\"type\":\"item.completed\",\"item\":{\"id\":\"item_47\",\"type\":\"agent_message\",\"text\":\"All eight cases now have native attempts underway or returned. It forbids installs, external actions, Git mutations, and additional workers.\\n\\n`capability-probes.json` retains executable discovery, exact command arguments, stdout, stderr, and exit codes. `uptime` reported load averages `14.54 9.00 7.41` during the first probe. `sysctl -n hw.logicalcpu` failed with exit 1 and `Operation not permitted`. Bun's `node:os.cpus()` returned 14 logical CPUs. That API result does not establish allocated CPU quota or prove an idle machine. The 12-minute ceiling was stated before inspection. No costly experiment was started.\\n\\n## All seven questions\\n\\n| Canonical question | Current finding | Required method |\\n| --- | --- | --- |\\n| 1. Why not double? | Unverified. There is no evidenced limiter. | Run a separate profile for the real export and map the hot path or resource counter to source. Check CPU, I/O, locks, and the load generator. Exclude profiling runs from reported timing samples. |\\n| 2. Was it tuned? | Unknown for both sides. | Use each side's production build and settings. Record runtime and dependency versions, batching, transactions, pools, indexes, and cache policy. Tune an avoidable configuration limiter and rerun before choosing an option. If tuning is unavailable, withhold an adoption winner. |\\n| 3. Did it break limits? | Cannot calculate a physical bound without elapsed time, bytes, bandwidth, CPU quota, or changed-path share. | Compute bytes divided by seconds against disk and network limits. Compare operations per second times CPU cost per operation against available core-seconds. Check the maximum saving permitted by the changed component's end-to-end share. A number beyond those bounds requires investigation of skipped work, caching, or bugs. |\\n| 4. Did it error? | Explicitly unknown. No error counter or correct-output check was supplied. | Record attempted, successful, rejected, failed, timed-out, and retried work for every sample. Verify exact expected row counts and content against an independent expected result. Output existence alone is insufficient. Add missing error counters to the actual runner within authorized scope. |\\n| 5. Does it reproduce? | No inspected raw samples. A single asserted run cannot establish repeatability. | After a declared warmup policy, run at least five unprofiled samples per side in alternating order, A1, B1, A2, B2, through A5, B5. Keep the same dataset, concurrency, machine, configuration, and cache policy. Report every sample, per-side median and min-to-max range. Treat a gap below run-to-run variation as no measurable difference. Use a rank-sum test or supported runner statistics for a close call. |\\n| 6. Does it matter? | Unknown whether the assertion times an entire user-visible export or a helper. | Measure the full path the user waits for with realistic data and concurrency. Include queueing and completion where relevant. Report any micro result as a share of that full duration. |\\n| 7. Did it even happen? | Explicitly unknown whether writes finished or occurred within the timed region. | Start timing before the intended export work and stop only after awaited processing, required commits, stream completion, and destination acknowledgment. Confirm the requested rows and bytes were written, and that the result was consumed. State whether durability or only acknowledgment defines completion. |\\n\\n## How the comparison would run\\n\\nFirst supply the exact A and B commit identifiers, runnable checkout locations, dependency versions, production configurations, dataset, expected output, concurrency, destination, and a measurement script. Read the script before executing it. Record what it times, counts, and ignores. Define completion and keep every write or await required by that definition inside the timed region. Confirm correctness and error counters on both sides before performance interpretation.\\n\\nRecord machine contention and CPU allocation. Use a declared warmup and cache policy matching production. Obtain the limiter from a separate profiling run. Then perform at least ten timing samples in alternating order, five for A and five for B. Capture raw durations in an explicit unit together with correctness, error, and completed-work counts for every sample. Keep profile overhead out of those samples.\\n\\nCalculate medians and ranges, compare the gap with observed variation, check physical limits, and measure the end-to-end export path if the original script only times part of it. Report faster, slower, no measurable difference, or inconclusive with the unit, sample count, range, and evidenced limiter. Missing limiter, untuned sides, unchecked errors, or unverified completed work keep an improvement claim inconclusive.\\n\\nThe one-run exception applies only when the user explicitly requests a quick ballpark. That report must say it is one run and still verify questions 4 and 7. It cannot choose between adoption options. This prompt requests an A/B improvement assessment and does not invoke that exception. Even a requested ballpark would remain unsupported here because errors and completed writes are unknown.\\n\\n## Where the evidence lives\\n\\nAll paths below are relative to this `outputs/worker` directory.\\n\\n- `inputs.json` contains full supplied assignment, input, and task contract texts with SHA-256 hashes.\\n- `rev-parse.json`, `status.json`, and `ls-tree.json` contain initial exact Git command arguments and results.\\n- `raw-command-records.json` contains a repeated revision check, read-only inventory, status, and uptime with exact arguments and unabridged stdout, stderr, and exit codes.\\n- `capability-probes.json` contains discovery and the failed CPU-count command as well as successful probes.\\n- `inspection.json` records the root inventory, absent selected fixtures, missing runner and A/B inputs, and Bun OS CPU observation.\\n- `source-reads.json` contains the exact workflow source paths, hashes, and inspected root documentation.\\n- `source-snapshot-hashes.json` hashes every saved instruction snapshot. The `.txt` files retain full source bytes, including mode, Principles index, benchmark checklist, applied principle leaves, and linked support.\\n\\nExplain the Number changed the decision to withhold the 30% improvement claim. The actual workload, data size, input distribution, concurrency, timed region, request scheduling, result consumption, success definition, and reporting statistic are absent. Some requests did not complete successfully. The count of successful requests that performed the full parse is unknown. Cache hits could have skipped parser work, but their frequency and distribution between sides are unknown.\\n\\nThe reported run count is one. It is unclear whether that means one comparison or one run for each side. No repetition, sample collection method, warmup policy, median, range, or spread is available. The 4x ratio has no supplied baseline or after values or units. I cannot recalculate it or normalize it by correct completed work.\\n\\n## Full benchmark checklist\\n\\n| Question | Evidence inspected and finding | Evidence needed to resolve it |\\n| --- | --- | --- |\\n| 1. Why not double? | No runtime profile or system counters were supplied. CPU, lock, disk, network, parser, and load-generator limits are all unverified. There is no observed hot path to map to source. | Capture a separate profiling run, process and system counters, and load-generator utilization. Map the observed limiter to the exact source revision. Keep profiled timings separate from reported timings. |\\n| 2. Was it tuned? | Exact A and B revisions and production configurations are absent. Tuning parity is unverified. | Record release builds, runtime versions, flags, environment, pools, batching, cache policy, identical data, and equivalent production tuning for both sides. Tune both before choosing a winner. |\\n| 3. Did it break limits? | No elapsed times, bytes, operations, core count, bandwidth, or baseline parser share were supplied. A quantitative physical-limit check is unavailable. | Compare measured bytes per second to disk and network capacity and CPU work to available cores. Measure the baseline share of the changed parser. |\\n| 4. Did it error? | The scenario explicitly reports failures, without counts, statuses, retries, timeouts, or validated outputs. Errors are not ruled out. Fast rejections can inflate apparent speed, while retries and timeouts can distort it in either direction. | Record attempted, successful, failed, timed-out, retried, and correctly completed work for each side. Validate output correctness and compare equivalent successful work. Retain failures separately. |\\n| 5. Does it reproduce? | One reported run provides no run-to-run spread. Warmup, contention, drift, and noise remain unresolved. | Run each side at least five times and alternate A and B. Retain all samples, report each side's median and range, and treat a gap smaller than variation as no measurable difference. For a close result, use appropriate statistics. |\\n| 6. Does it matter? | No user-visible end-to-end timings, realistic workload, or parser share were supplied. End-to-end relevance is unverified. | Measure the complete request path with realistic sizes and concurrency. Report the micro result alongside the parser's fraction of total latency. |\\n| 7. Did it even happen? | Failed requests and uncounted cache hits undermine proof that every timed request executed the parser and consumed its output. No timed-region trace or completed-work counters are present. | Instrument parser invocation and completion, cache hits and misses, actual input bytes, and consumed correct outputs. Verify awaited completion and that the work occurs inside the timer. |\\n\\nThe physical-limit arithmetic is a conditional check, not a measurement of this case. If only a fraction f of baseline time changes, even making that part instantaneous gives a maximum whole-path speedup of 1 divided by 1 minus f. A whole-path 4x improvement from that change alone therefore requires f to be at least 0.75. The parser's actual share is unknown. A small function need not have a small runtime share. Neither assumption proves or disproves the reported ratio.\\n\\n## Alternatives that remain unresolved\\n\\nFailures, skipped work, cache bypass, unequal inputs, unequal tuning, warmup, machine contention, load-generator saturation, timer placement, discarded or unawaited results, and a micro improvement too small to affect the whole request remain possible explanations. The scenario already establishes that errors and cache accounting need correction. It does not establish which cause produced the ratio.\\n\\n## Measurement plan and report gates\\n\\nThe candidate claim to test is that the changed parser completes the same correct uncached parsing work four times as fast under matched production settings. That claim needs an explicit metric and unit. If the intended claim concerns whole requests, measure that separately.\\n\\nFirst recover the actual A/B parser revisions, harness, data, configurations, and raw report. Read the harness to identify what it times, counts, and ignores. Record workload, data size, concurrency, timed region, and sample count. Observe load and core count on the actual benchmark machine before measurement. If unrelated contention cannot be removed within authorization, interleave sides and disclose it. Checking this worker's machine now would not reconstruct the original run.\\n\\nFix accounting for failures and cache hits, validate results, align production tuning and cache policies, then repeat matched A/B runs. Profile separately and map the observed limiter to source. Preserve raw outputs, counters, errors, run order, median, range, revisions, and the end-to-end comparison with any eventual number. Until questions 1, 2, 4, and 7 are resolved, retain the inconclusive verdict.
